=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
In the nation of AtCoder, there are N cities numbered 1 to N, and M roads numbered 1 to M.
Road i connects cities A_i and B_i bidirectionally and has a length of C_i.
You are given Q queries to process in order. The queries are of the following two types.

- 1 i: Road i becomes closed.
- 2 x y: Print the shortest distance from city x to city y, using only roads that are not closed. If city y cannot be reached from city x, print -1 instead.

It is guaranteed that each test case contains at most 300 queries of the first type.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M Q
A_1 B_1 C_1
\vdots
A_M B_M C_M
\mathrm{query}_1
\vdots
\mathrm{query}_Q

Each query is in one of the following two formats:
1 i

2 x y

Output

Process the queries in order.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 300
- 0 \leq M \leq \frac{N(N-1)}{2}
- 1 \leq A_i < B_i \leq N
- All pairs (A_i, B_i) are distinct.
- 1 \leq C_i \leq 10^9
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- In the queries of the first type, 1 \leq i \leq M.
- The road given in a query of the first type is not already closed at that time.
- The number of queries of the first type is at most 300.
- In the queries of the second type, 1 \leq x < y \leq N.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 3 5
1 2 5
1 3 10
2 3 6
2 1 3
1 2
2 1 3
1 1
2 1 3

Sample Output 1

10
11
-1


- In the first query, print the shortest distance from city 1 to city 3, which is 10.
- In the second query, road 2 becomes closed.
- In the third query, print the shortest distance from city 1 to city 3, which is 11.
- In the fourth query, road 1 becomes closed.
- In the fifth query, city 3 cannot be reached from city 1, so print -1.

Sample Input 2

4 6 6
2 3 1
2 4 1
3 4 1
1 2 1
1 3 1
1 4 1
1 4
1 5
1 6
2 1 2
2 1 3
2 1 4

Sample Output 2

-1
-1
-1

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Implementation for add_edge:
def add_edge(u, v, w):
    old_u = dist[u]
    old_v = dist[v]
    # But if we assign dist[u] later, old_u references row object; if we update row in-place, old_u changes! Need copy.
    old_u = dist[u][:]
    old_v = dist[v][:]
    for a in range(N):
        row = dist[a]
        du = row[u]
        dv = row[v]
        if du == INF and dv == INF:
            continue
        if du != INF and dv != INF:
            bu = du + w
            bv = dv + w
            for b in range(N):
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd
        elif du != INF:
            bu = du + w
            for b in range(N):
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd
        else:  # dv != INF
            bv = dv + w
            for b in range(N):
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd

But note if du != INF and dv != INF, and old_v[b] or old_u[b] INF, nd > INF; no update. But if row[b] is INF, nd > INF, no update. Good.

Now, let's evaluate performance of add_edge with snapshots and combined loop. We can localize variables for speed:
def add_edge(u, v, w):
    d = dist
    n = N
    inf = INF
    old_u = d[u][:]
    old_v = d[v][:]
    for a in range(n):
        row = d[a]
        du = row[u]
        dv = row[v]
        if du == inf and dv == inf:
            continue
        if du != inf and dv != inf:
            bu = du + w
            bv = dv + w
            for b in range(n):
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd
        elif du != inf:
            bu = du + w
            for b in range(n):
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd
        else:
            bv = dv + w
            for b in range(n):
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < row[b]:
                    row[b] = nd
But note if du != inf and dv != inf, and old_v[b] or old_u[b] inf, nd > inf. No update. Fine.

In Floyd, use range_N.

Now, add_edge:
def add_edge(u, v, w):
    d = dist
    n = N
    inf = INF
    old_u = d[u][:]
    old_v = d[v][:]
    for a in range_N:
        row = d[a]
        du = row[u]
        dv = row[v]
        if du == inf and dv == inf:
            continue
        if du != inf and dv != inf:
            bu = du + w
            bv = dv + w
            for b in range_N:
                rb = row[b]
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd
                    rb = nd
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd
        elif du != inf:
            bu = du + w
            for b in range_N:
                rb = row[b]
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd
        else:
            bv = dv + w
            for b in range_N:
                rb = row[b]
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd

Note: In combined branch, if first update changes row[b], rb updated; second update compares to new rb. Good. If first update doesn't, rb old. Good.

Potential issue: In combined branch, if du != inf and dv != inf, but bu + old_v[b] and bv + old_u[b] both > INF? no update. Good.

Now, add_edge:
```
def add_edge(u, v, w):
    d = dist
    rng = range_N
    inf = INF
    old_u = d[u][:]
    old_v = d[v][:]
    for a in rng:
        row = d[a]
        du = row[u]
        dv = row[v]
        if du == inf and dv == inf:
            continue
        if du != inf and dv != inf:
            bu = du + w
            bv = dv + w
            for b in rng:
                rb = row[b]
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd
                    rb = nd
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd
        elif du != inf:
            bu = du + w
            for b in rng:
                rb = row[b]
                nd = bu + old_v[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd
        else:
            bv = dv + w
            for b in rng:
                rb = row[b]
                nd = bv + old_u[b]
                if nd < rb:
                    row[b] = nd
```
This is good.

Could we reduce duplicate code by using a helper? No, function call inside loop bad.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of `dist` being modified by add_edge and then another type1; add_edge uses
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    N, M, Q = map(int, input().split())

    roads = [None] * (M + 1)
    for i in range(1, M + 1):
        a, b, c = map(int, input().split())
        roads[i] = (a - 1, b - 1, c)

    queries = []
    closed = [False] * (M + 1)
    has_type2 = False

    for _ in range(Q):
        parts = list(map(int, input().split()))
        if parts[0] == 1:
            i = parts[1]
            queries.append((1, i, 0))
            closed[i] = True
        else:
            x = parts[1] - 1
            y = parts[2] - 1
            queries.append((2, x, y))
            has_type2 = True

    if not has_type2:
        return

    INF = 10**18
    dist = [[INF] * N for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(N):
        dist[i][i] = 0

    for i in range(1, M + 1):
        if not closed[i]:
            a, b, c = roads[i]
            if c < dist[a][b]:
                dist[a][b] = c
                dist[b][a] = c

    range_N = range(N)

    # Floyd-Warshall on the final graph (all roads that are never closed)
    for k in range_N:
        dk = dist[k]
        for i in range_N:
            if i == k:
                continue
            row = dist[i]
            dik = row[k]
            if dik == INF:
                continue
            for j in range_N:
                rj = row[j]
                nd = dik + dk[j]
                if nd < rj:
                    row[j] = nd

    def add_edge(u, v, w):
        d = dist
        rng = range_N
        inf = INF

        old_u = d[u][:]
        old_v = d[v][:]

        for a in rng:
            row = d[a]
            du = row[u]
            dv = row[v]

            if du == inf and dv == inf:
                continue

            if du != inf and dv != inf:
                bu = du + w
                bv = dv + w
                for b in rng:
                    rb = row[b]
                    nd = bu + old_v[b]
                    if nd < rb:
                        row[b] = nd
                        rb = nd
                    nd = bv + old_u[b]
                    if nd < rb:
                        row[b] = nd
            elif du != inf:
                bu = du + w
                for b in rng:
                    rb = row[b]
                    nd = bu + old_v[b]
                    if nd < rb:
                        row[b] = nd
            else:
                bv = dv + w
                for b in rng:
                    rb = row[b]
                    nd = bv + old_u[b]
                    if nd < rb:
                        row[b] = nd

    ans = []

    # Process queries backwards: closed roads become open.
    for typ, a, b in reversed(queries):
        if typ == 1:
            u, v, w = roads[a]
            add_edge(u, v, w)
        else:
            d = dist[a][b]
            ans.append(-1 if d == INF else d)

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(map(str, reversed(ans))))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
